abstract_pt: 'Os recentes avanços em  inteligência artificial (AI) e em tecnologias
  de sensores têm melhorado significativamente as soluções de prognóstico e  de avaliação
  de saúde, baseadas em dados, permitindo obter estimativas mais precisas da saúde
  das máquinas em diversos setores industriais. Ao integrar modelos de monitorização,
  os processos de manutenção podem ser otimizados para detetar antecipadamente condições
  de deterioração das máquinas, prevenindo eficazmente a ocorrência de falhas. A manutenção
  preditiva, que tem como objetivo estimar a vida útil restante (RUL) de uma máquina,
  minimiza operações de manutenção desnecessárias. Deste modo, as intervenções são
  apenas agendadas quando a RUL atinge um limiar crítico. Essa abordagem é especialmente
  valorizada em indústrias com altos custos de manutenção, como o setor de transportes,
  onde é crucial mitigar falhas para asegurar o funcionamento dos equipamentos.O desempenho
  dos modelos preditivos  depende significativamente de serem treinados com conjuntos
  de dados provenientes de diferentes máquinas que operaram sob diversas condições
  de funcionamento, especialmente durante as transições de estados saudáveis para
  estados não saudáveis. No entanto, a recolha de un número representativo de exemplos
  de degradação da saúde das máquinas é um desafio devido aos riscos significativos
  para a segurança, particularmente no setor dos transportes. Consequentemente, as
  empresas que supervisionam máquinas semelhantes estão a explorar cada vez mais iniciativas
  colaborativas de partilha de dados para desenvolver modelos de monitorização mais
  robustos. Para cumprir com os regulamentos de privacidade de dados, indústria e
  academia estão a desenvolver estratégias de agregação de conhecimento que preservam
  as informações sensíveis na sua origem.A aprendizagem federada (FL) tem surgido
  como uma abordagem de aprendizagem computacional (ML) descentralizada e preservadora
  da privacidade, ideal para cenários colaborativos com diversas configurações de
  partição de dados. Na FL, um desafio chave é lidar com a partição híbrida de dados,
  onde ocorrem variações tanto nos espaços de amostras quanto de características entre
  as partes, algo comum em muitas aplicações. Este desafio é ampliado ao tentar agregar
  conhecimento de modelos de ML com arquiteturas diferentes, devido a discrepâncias
  nos espaços de entrada. Tais questões são particularmente proeminentes em prognósticos
  colaborativos, onde máquinas equivalentes podem ter configurações de sensores diferentes.
  Com base na utilização extensiva de redes neuronais artificiais em aprendizagem
  federada e na monitorização de saúde das máquinas, esta tese apresenta a FedLabSync
  – uma solução federada projetada para a transição de abordagens centralizadas para
  descentralizadas de dados. A FedLabSync emprega uma estratégia de agregação de conhecimento,
  sincronizando amostras semelhantes com base nas suas etiquetas durante o treino
  dos modelos. Explorando essa abordagem, esta tese apresenta o FedMulLabSync, uma
  solução mais adaptável que incorpora estratégias de sincronização de etiquetas num
  espaço multidimensional, lidando de forma eficaz com dados não independentes e não
  identicamente distribuídos.Esta tese avaliou rigorosamente a viabilidade de soluções
  colaborativas em dois problemas de ML: classificação e regressão. Com foco nas indústrias
  de aviação e marítima, estas tarefas envolveram a deteção de anomalias em navios
  e a estimativa da RUL de aeronaves. Os resultados demonstraram que o desempenho
  dos modelos preditivos  das máquinas  de uma frota pode melhorar significativamente
  quando integrados numa rede colaborativa. Assim,  o método proposto nesta tese,  FedMulLabSync,
  tem potencial para vir a ser aplicado em outra áreas, para além dos prognósticos
  e da avaliação e monitorização de saúde das máquinas.'

Raúl Homero Llasag Rosero

ix

Abstract
A
dvances in artificial intelligence and sensing technologies have significantly
enhanced data-driven prognostics and health management solutions,
enabling more accurate estimation of machine health across diverse industrial
sectors. By integrating condition-monitoring models, maintenance processes
can be optimized to detect early signs of deterioration, effectively preventing
potential failures or disasters. Predictive maintenance, which focus on
estimating a machine’s Remaining Useful Life (RUL), minimizes unnecessary
maintenance by scheduling interventions when the RUL reaches a critical
threshold. This approach is especially valued in safety-critical industries with
high maintenance costs, such as transportation.
The predictive performance of data-driven models depends significantly on
training with diverse datasets from multiple machines operating under varying
conditions, especially during the transition from healthy to unhealthy states.
However, collecting extensive examples of machine health degradation using
a run-to-failure approach is challenging due to the significant safety risks,
particularly in transportation. As a result, companies overseeing similar
machines are increasingly exploring collaborative data-sharing initiatives to
develop more robust condition-monitoring models. To comply with data privacy
regulations organizations are focusing on knowledge-aggregation strategies that
preserve sensitive information at its source while enabling shared insights across
the industry.
Federated Learning (FL) has emerged as a privacy-preserving, decentralized
Machine Learning (ML) technique, ideally suited for collaborative scenarios with
diverse data partitioning settings. A key challenge is addressing hybrid data
partitioning, where variations occur both in sample and feature spaces across
parties, which is common in many applications. This challenge is amplified
when trying to aggregate knowledge from ML models with different architectures
due to discrepancies in input spaces. Such issues are particularly prominent in
collaborative prognostics, where equivalent machines may have different sensor
configurations.
Building on the extensive use of artificial neural networks in FL and health
monitoring, this thesis introduces FedLabSync—a federated solution designed to
shift from centralized to decentralized data approaches. FedLabSync employs a
knowledge aggregation strategy by synchronizing similar samples based on their
labels during AI model training. Expanding on this foundation, this thesis
presents FedMulLabSync, a more adaptable solution that incorporates label
synchronization strategies within a multidimensional space, effectively handling
non-independent and non-identically distributed (non-i.i.d) data.
This thesis rigorously assessed the feasibility of collaborative solutions in two
common ML tasks: classification and regression. Focusing on the aviation and
maritime industries, these tasks involved anomaly detection in ships and RUL
estimation of aircraft. The results demonstrated that machines within a fleet
xi

can notably improve their AI models’ predictive performance when connected
through a collaborative network. These findings suggest that FedMulLabSync
has the potential for broader applications beyond prognostics and health
management.
Keywords: Prognostics and Health Management, Predictive Maintenance,
Remaining Useful Life, Federated Learning, Machine Learning
xii

Resumo
O
s recentes avanços em inteligência artificial (AI) e em tecnologias de sensores
têm melhorado significativamente as soluções de prognóstico e de avaliação
de saúde, baseadas em dados, permitindo obter estimativas mais precisas da
saúde das máquinas em diversos setores industriais. Ao integrar modelos
de monitorização, os processos de manutenção podem ser otimizados para
detetar antecipadamente condições de deterioração das máquinas, prevenindo
eficazmente a ocorrência de falhas. A manutenção preditiva, que tem como
objetivo estimar a vida útil restante (RUL) de uma máquina, minimiza
operações de manutenção desnecessárias. Deste modo, as intervenções são
apenas agendadas quando a RUL atinge um limiar crítico. Essa abordagem é
especialmente valorizada em indústrias com altos custos de manutenção, como o
setor de transportes, onde é crucial mitigar falhas para asegurar o funcionamento
dos equipamentos.
O desempenho dos modelos preditivos depende significativamente de serem
treinados com conjuntos de dados provenientes de diferentes máquinas que
operaram sob diversas condições de funcionamento, especialmente durante as
transições de estados saudáveis para estados não saudáveis. No entanto, a
recolha de un número representativo de exemplos de degradação da saúde
das máquinas é um desafio devido aos riscos significativos para a segurança,
particularmente no setor dos transportes. Consequentemente, as empresas
que supervisionam máquinas semelhantes estão a explorar cada vez mais
iniciativas colaborativas de partilha de dados para desenvolver modelos de
monitorização mais robustos. Para cumprir com os regulamentos de privacidade
de dados, indústria e academia estão a desenvolver estratégias de agregação de
conhecimento que preservam as informações sensíveis na sua origem.
A aprendizagem federada (FL) tem surgido como uma abordagem
de aprendizagem computacional (ML) descentralizada e preservadora da
privacidade, ideal para cenários colaborativos com diversas configurações de
partição de dados. Na FL, um desafio chave é lidar com a partição híbrida
de dados, onde ocorrem variações tanto nos espaços de amostras quanto de
características entre as partes, algo comum em muitas aplicações. Este desafio
é ampliado ao tentar agregar conhecimento de modelos de ML com arquiteturas
diferentes, devido a discrepâncias nos espaços de entrada. Tais questões são
particularmente proeminentes em prognósticos colaborativos, onde máquinas
equivalentes podem ter configurações de sensores diferentes. Com base na
utilização extensiva de redes neuronais artificiais em aprendizagem federada e
na monitorização de saúde das máquinas, esta tese apresenta a FedLabSync –
uma solução federada projetada para a transição de abordagens centralizadas
para descentralizadas de dados. A FedLabSync emprega uma estratégia de
agregação de conhecimento, sincronizando amostras semelhantes com base nas
suas etiquetas durante o treino dos modelos. Explorando essa abordagem, esta
tese apresenta o FedMulLabSync, uma solução mais adaptável que incorpora
xiii

estratégias de sincronização de etiquetas num espaço multidimensional, lidando
de forma eficaz com dados não independentes e não identicamente distribuídos.
Esta tese avaliou rigorosamente a viabilidade de soluções colaborativas em
dois problemas de ML: classificação e regressão. Com foco nas indústrias
de aviação e marítima, estas tarefas envolveram a deteção de anomalias em
navios e a estimativa da RUL de aeronaves. Os resultados demonstraram que o
desempenho dos modelos preditivos das máquinas de uma frota pode melhorar
significativamente quando integrados numa rede colaborativa. Assim, o método
proposto nesta tese, FedMulLabSync, tem potencial para vir a ser aplicado em
outra áreas, para além dos prognósticos e da avaliação e monitorização de saúde
das máquinas.
Palavras-chave: prognóstico e gestão de saúde, manutenção
preditiva, vida útil restante, aprendizagem computacional,
aprendizagem federada.

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
A
rtificial Intelligence ( AI) has emerged as a key enabler of progress in
the 21st century, empowering future society and industry development.
With the widespread adoption of monitoring sensors (e.g., vibration, shock,
temperature and others), business strategies use data analysis and AI in machine
health monitoring for improving maintenance processes. A study of ( Biggio
& Kastanis , 2020) composed of more than 230 European business interviews
demonstrated that more than 90% adopt unproductive policies. In response,
companies demonstrated interest in adopting condition-based and predictive
maintenance solutions ( Berthelsen, Goodness & Sharpington , 2021).
Condition-Based Maintenance ( CBM) employs predictive models to instantly
alert maintenance teams when health indicators deviate from desired values,
relying primarily on data analysis. Predictive Maintenance ( PdM) employs
prognosis models to anticipate when maintenance activities should be
performed. Unlike reactive, scheduled, and condition-based maintenance
strategies, predictive maintenance utilizes techniques to estimate the useful
lifetime of mechanical or infrastructure assets to determine the optimal time for
maintenance interventions. Gartner predicts that by 2026, 60% of PdM solutions
assisted by monitoring sensors will be delivered as part of enterprise equipment
management to reduce operational downtime and increase its reliability. In
response, research studies have been focused on establishing PdM practical
guidelines for industrial applications.
Gartner strongly advocates for the adoption of data-driven models, both
AI-based and statistics-based, by leveraging sensor data collected within the
framework of Condition Monitoring ( CM) principles. These principles entail
measuring specific machine’s parameters to swiftly detect any significant changes
that might signal potential failures (MAA, 2014). This approach gains additional
strength through the advances in AI. Therefore, this approach underscores the
seamless integration of AI into PdM solutions. Thanks to AI, engineers are
relieved from the necessity of fully comprehending the monitored environment, as
Machine Learning ( ML) algorithms, particularly Deep Learning ( DL) methods,
learn from extensive datasets.
The main constraint for developing AI/ML prognosis models is related to
the availability of data with numerous run-to-failure examples, where a single
example refers to the health degradation trajectory of the monitored machine
(Arias Chao et al. , 2021). In other words, run-to-failure datasets comprise a set
of run-to-failure trajectories or multi-sensor time series related to time-to-failure
indicators. Then, these datasets are used to train models to estimate the time
that maintenance interventions should be performed. The widely accepted way
to calculate this time refers to employing the Remaining Useful Life ( RUL),
a metric referring to the amount of time left before the system health of a
determined asset falls below a defined failure threshold ( Saxena et al. , 2008).
— 2 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
1.1 Motivation
Successful implementation of predictive maintenance hinges on precise RUL
estimations. The efficacy of this maintenance strategy is closely linked to timely
identification of potential failures or performance limitations within previously
specified limits ( MAA, 2014). In the development of RUL estimators, the
research community delegates the success of AI/ML solutions to the quantity
and quality of run-to-failure trajectories. However, companies have encountered
challenges in collecting numerous trajectories and covering a wide spectrum
of operating conditions, particularly when machine availability is crucial for
guaranteeing human safety such in transport industry ( Adrià Salvador et al. ,
2019; Yaguo et al. , 2018). Transport companies have demonstrated interest in
adopting collaborative prognosis techniques for enabling a network of multiple
parties to learn from each other, therefore developing significant prognosis
models ( Dhada, Jain, Herrera et al. , 2020).
Concerns regarding data privacy have led to companies’ apprehension
regarding collaborative solutions based on data centralization. In response,
data-privacy legislation has emerged to regulate the development of
data-decentralized applications in the world, e.g, the General Data Protection
Regulation (GDPR 1) from European Union, the California Consumer Privacy
Act (CCPA 2) from the USA, and the Personal Data Protection Act (PDPA 3)
from Singapore.
In response to the challenge posed by legislative regulations concerning
raw data transmission, Google introduced Federated Learning ( FL) in 2016
(McMahan, Moore & Ramage , 2016). This privacy-preserving and decentralized
approach revolutionized ML by allowing sensitive data to remain on the
devices of data producers while collaboratively training predictive models.
Federated learning has become a cornerstone of decentralized ML by reducing
data communication overhead. It fact lies in its effective application of
ML methods utilizing stochastic optimization for aggregating knowledge from
multiple predictive models.
The popularity of FL has grown exponentially since its conception, while
studies predict that it will continue increasing until there may be mature
solutions ( Q. Li, Wen, Wu et al. , 2019). As evidence, the global FL market was
valued at $110.82 million in 2021 and is expected to grow to 210 million in 2028
(Polaris, 2022). Over this market, solutions towards different data partitioning
settings have been built ( Abdulrahman et al. , 2021).
From a ML perspective, the difference between data partitioning settings
lies in how samples and features of a joined dataset are naturally distributed
among data producers or parties. Horizontal data partition comprises parties’
datasets differing in sample space, while vertical data partition comprises parties’
datasets differing in feature space. Unfortunately, none of the previous data
partitioning settings address the partial unavailability of monitoring sensors or
sample features for parties with versatile collaborative prognosis problems.
1https://gdpr-info.eu/
2https://oag.ca.gov/privacy/ccpa
3https://sso.agc.gov.sg/Act/PDPA2012
— 3 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
1.2 Problem statement
This research on collaborative prognosis through Federated Learning ( FL)
focuses on addressing emerging challenges within the aviation industry. This
industry was chosen because aircraft are widely regarded as some of the most
complex transport with expensive maintenance costs ever constructed. Thus
any advancements made in FL within this sector are poised to deliver significant
value across other industries monitoring machines via sensing technologies.
Airlines are interested in acquiring highly accurate predictive maintenance
solutions not only because they incur significant maintenance expenses but
also because they grapple with the imperative to boost fleet availability and
reliability. To do that, minimizing unnecessary inspections and aircraft on
ground (AOG) instances becomes crucial—a term denoting issues severe enough
to ground an aircraft ( F AA, 2018).
In 2018, the International Air Transport Association ( IATA) estimated
that global aircraft Maintenance, Repair and Operations ( MRO) costs,
encompassing facilities capable of conducting necessary maintenance and
inspections, accounted for 9% of total operational expenses for airlines ( IATA,
2019). With the forecasted growth of aircraft ( Technik, 2020), the MRO
market that supports it is also expected to grow. Without relevant advances
in predictive maintenance, the MRO costs are expected to rise to $116 billion
by 2029, up from $81.9 billion in 2019 ( Cooper et al. , 2019).
As aircraft health monitoring plays a crucial role in minimizing MRO costs,
airlines are confronted with bolstering the reliability of aircraft systems and
components. This challenge necessitates adopting predictive maintenance
strategies and AI models to leverage the onboard condition monitoring sensors
found in modern aircraft, aiming to reduce operational downtime effectively. In
this context, the research problem addressed in this work is stated as follows:
Research Problem. Developing collaborative solutions adaptable to different
data partitioning settings while ensuring privacy compliance is essential for
integrating knowledge from equivalent machines whose monitoring systems
may present partial sensor unavailability. This research addresses the gap in
federated approaches for creating accurate Remaining Useful Life estimators for
these machines, overcoming challenges related to the scarcity of run-to-failure
trajectories and the need to cover a wide range of operating conditions across a
fleet with discrepancies in sensor configurations.
1.3 Research questions
The adoption of Federated Learning, a decentralized ML approach, is explored
for solving this research problem corresponding to a collaborative prognosis
problem with data privacy requirements. Performance prediction improvements
on the RUL estimation of aircraft systems in individual airlines are expected by
constructing a collaborative scenario. In this logic, the general research question
is formulated as follows:
— 4 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
General Question. Can Federated Learning perform the Remaining Useful Life
estimation of machines, such as aircraft systems, when equivalent monitoring
systems in a fleet vary in sensor availability?
Establishing a baseline prognosis model from a data-centralized approach has
been essential for gauging the expected performance of RUL estimation during
the design and implementation of federated or data-decentralized solutions.
While a baseline is crucial for improving the prediction performance of federated
AI models, it is important to note that a centralized approach does not comply
with data privacy policies and is only suitable for proof-of-concept experiments.
Given the challenges of developing prognosis models for estimating the RUL
of aircraft systems even in a data-centralized approach, this work first aims to
answer the following research questions:
RQ1 How can the degradation of machines be represented to enable reliable
prediction of their Remaining Useful Life?
RQ2 How do evaluate the feasibility of collaborative solutions at different data
partitioning settings?
RQ3 How can federated learning aggregate knowledge from prognosis models
developed with condition monitoring data from equivalent machines whose
monitoring systems 1) do not and 2) differ in monitoring sensors?
1.4 Research goals and outcomes
This research aims to integrate knowledge from multiple PdM models developed
using condition monitoring data from equivalent aircraft systems whose
monitoring systems may differ in monitoring sensors. This challenge aims
to be addressed by proposing novel federated algorithms utilizing stochastic
optimization for aggregating knowledge from multiple predictive models. In
this way, run-to-failure data from Boeing aircraft cooling units is considered due
to its availability for the ReMAP project. Complementary, the use of benchmark
data such Commercial Modular Aero-Propulsion System Simulation ( C-MAPSS)
datasets are considered due to the vast available literature and public availability.
To achieve the main goal of this research work and in order to collaboratively
improve the RUL estimation of equivalent aircraft systems, the following
research goals emerged:
RG1 Develop AI models in a data-centralized approach to predict theRUL of
machines. This research aims to address the challenge of representing
the health degradation of aircraft systems by centralizing all condition
monitoring data on a server, as outlined in research question RQ1.
RG2 Design and implementFL algorithms to aggregateRUL estimation models
trained with data from equivalent machines whose monitoring systems
may differ in monitoring sensors. This research seeks to address research
questions RQ2 and RQ3 by outlining strategies for aggregating knowledge
from AI models and evaluating their feasibility.
— 5 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
RG3 Describe knowledge aggregation strategies to improve theRUL estimation
in data-decentralized approaches. This research aims to describe the
strategies used to enhance the RUL estimation on prognosis models
developed in collaborative scenarios, thereby paving the way for the
industry of the future.
1.5 Contributions
Considering the research goals outlined above, integrating predictive
maintenance with Federated Learning involves transitioning from
data-centralized to data-decentralized approaches, which introduces various
alternatives in between. This thesis supports researchers in effectively
navigating this transition by offering the following contributions detailed in
scientific publications.
• A model development pipeline that guides the development of
data-driven models while allows the combination of physics-based and
AI-based approaches. This pipeline, encompassing data acquisition,
data pre-processing, and model fitting stages, is instrumental in
constructing health indicators from physics-based models when health
degradation insights are difficult to obtain using simply AI-based methods.
Particularly, this pipeline, described in scientific publication J3, guides
researchers in finding health indicators from signal transformations in time
and/or frequency domains, which serve as input parameters for an AI/ML
model.
• The construction of health indicators by extracting information from
time and frequency domains of sensor signals. This process, detailed in
scientific publication J3, is achieved by mathematically decomposing a
signal into intrinsic mode functions and applying the Hilbert transform
to compute instantaneous frequencies. These health indicators are
particularly useful for uncovering health degradation insights, essential
for estimating the RUL of Boeing aircraft cooling units. Therefore, they
serve as a clear example addressing research question RQ1, demonstrating
that when data transformations in the time or frequency domains are
insufficient to reveal signs of health degradation, the development of hybrid
models can be effective.
• RUL estimation models for Boeing aircraft cooling units and
turbofan engines developed under data-centralized approaches in
scientific publications J1, J2, J3, C1, C3, C4 and C5. Developing
accurate prognostic models has proven to be challenging, even with
data-centralized approaches. The referenced publications describe various
ML methods adopted by participating parties, which evaluate their
feasibility using subsets of the available data that shrink as the number
of parties increases. During the selection of the ML method and its
architecture, there is a clear preference for neural networks.
— 6 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
• A federated evaluation framework adaptable to different federated
algorithms that may configure different data partitioning settings. This
framework, adopted in all experiments presented in this thesis, emerges
to guide researchers in conducting appropriate comparisons to answer
RQ2 and the following questions: How well do predictive models from
data-isolated approaches perform on data experienced by other parties?
Do federated models achieve prediction performance comparable to a
data-centralized approach? And, do federated models collaboratively
enhance estimation performance within the federated approach?. This
contribution alleviates researchers’ time spent on experiment design,
allowing them to focus on addressing other challenges of interest, such
as improving the prediction performance of AI/ML models.
• Solutions for collaborative prognosis problems when parties
datasets overlap in feature space , which are pertinent to achieving
research goal RG2. Using C-MAPSS datasets and federated algorithms
using stochastic optimization, the competence of a variety of neural
network methods has been evaluated in scientific publications J1,
J2, C1, C2 and C4 for estimating the RUL of aircraft systems.
The competitiveness analysis discusses the challenges associated with
collaborative problem construction through data partitioning and
highlights the limitations of neural network families by comparing
their prediction performance. Various neural networks were compared
to contribute to research goal RG3, which focuses on improving
the prediction performance of RUL estimators in data-decentralized
approaches. The prediction performance of some neural networks was
comparable with existing ML methods in literature.
• A baseline federated algorithm is proposed for solving
collaborative problems where parties’ datasets may differ in
both sample and feature spaces , contributing to research goals
RG2 and RG3, and addressing research question RQ2. The federated
algorithm from scientific publication J2, named FedLabSync, trains a
neural network using a collaborative and penalized stochastic optimization
approach. The output target synchronization strategy employed by
this algorithm aggregates knowledge from multiple parties, enabling
collaborative forecasting or classification problems and leveraging the
promising capabilities of neural networks. This strategy addresses the
need for algorithms to tackle predictive problems using neural network
families, particularly when parties’ datasets involve data partitioning in
feature and sample spaces.
• The competitiveness analysis of FedLabSync conducted by
comparing its prediction performance with well-known algorithms
designed for benchmark data partitioning settings: horizontal and vertical.
This comparison, described in scientific publication J2, is assessed in two
industrial application scenarios: failure prediction in production lines and
the estimation of the remaining useful life of aircraft systems. Experiments
— 7 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
have shown that FedLabSync can address collaborative problems involving
data partitioning in feature and sample spaces. Furthermore, it has
improved the prediction performance of some collaborative parties.
• The description of knowledge aggregation strategies used in
collaborative problems with different data partitioning settings, e.g.,
model averaging, index synchronization, label synchronization and others.
Specifically, FedMulLabSync from scientific publication J1 has been
extended from FedLabSync to incorporate these label synchronization
strategies, addressing the data partitioning settings in federated learning.
These strategies rely on the correlation of similar samples across two
key dimensions: 1) unidimensional output targets and 2) sample
representation within a reduced space, which masks their original meaning.
Consequently, this contribution aligns with the objectives outlined in
research goal RG3.
• Public GitHub repositories containing collaborative prognosis
experiments with public data from turbofan engines from NASA. Those
repositories, available at Appendix A, contain code in Matlab and Python
programming languages.
1.6 Thesis outline
This thesis details the contributions summarized above across seven chapters.
Below is an outline:
• Chapter 2 presents background material on aircraft health monitoring,
which promotes the adoption of novel maintenance strategies. Particularly,
this chapter describes the process, challenges and concepts needed to
develop data-driven Remaining Useful Life ( RUL) estimators for aircraft
systems, crucial for answering research question RQ1.
• Chapter 3 introduces Federated Learning as a privacy-preserving and
decentralized Machine Learning ( ML) approach to solve collaborative
problems. After the benefits, components and key concepts, this chapter
characterizes federated algorithms and knowledge aggregation strategies to
answer research questions RQ2 and RQ3. This characterization guides
solving collaborative problems where participant parties may configure
different data partitions. Particularly, this chapter details federated
algorithms compatible with ML methods using stochastic optimization,
such as artificial neural networks.
• Chapter 4 describes the challenges associated with constructing RUL
estimators using data from aircraft cooling units and turbofan engines to
answer RQ1. Before explaining these predictive models’ architecture and
prediction performance, this chapter introduces a PHM pipeline designed
to construct robust data-driven models.
— 8 —

CHAPTER 1. INTRODUCTION
• Chapter 5 adopts Federated Learning for solving collaborative problems.
In this way, a federated evaluation framework is described to guide
researchers in conducting appropriate evaluation experiments to answer
research question RQ2. By proposing so, this chapter is mainly dedicated
to answering research question RQ3 by describing the techniques used
to aggregate prognosis models developed with data from aircraft systems
whose monitoring systems may differ in monitoring sensors. To answer
sub-questions of RQ3, differing in the distribution of monitoring sensors,
this chapter describes FedLabSync—a federated solution that synchronizes
the labels of participant parties to aggregate knowledge of their AI/ML
models trained using stochastic optimisation.
• Chapter 6 primarily evaluates the competitiveness of FedLabSync and
its extended version, FedMulLabSync. It explores their application
in scenarios beyond the aviation industry, such as failure detection in
production lines and anomaly detection in maritime engines. This chapter
introduces new knowledge aggregation strategies for FedMulLabSync
based on multidimensional label synchronization, specifically time-sharing
and similarity-based synchronization. Both strategies contribute to
answering RQ3. Time-sharing expands the label space by incorporating
temporal information, while similarity-based synchronization computes
the similarity between multidimensional target labels from different
parties.
• Chapter 7 presents conclusions, outlines the limitations of the thesis, and
proposes directions for future work.
— 9 —
